<image-desc>
A dark blue leather book cover featuring elaborate gold-tooled (gilt) decoration. The design includes a double-rule border enclosing a wide decorative band of repeating floral and scroll motifs. At each of the four corners is a square compartment containing a large, stylized fleur-de-lis. Within the main gilded border, a scalloped or "dentelle" frame is impressed into the leather. The central panel of the cover is left plain, showcasing the grain of the dark blue leather. On the bottom left, near the spine, a small white paper library label is visible with the characters "Q." and "7" handwritten or printed on it.
</image-desc>

<page-type>preface</page-type>
<header>Number in Catalogue XVI.</header>
<warning>The text is written on a separate sheet of paper pasted onto the flyleaf; the right edge of this paper is torn and irregular, though the text remains legible.</warning>

<insert>
This most singular work is the production of Messahalah
an Arabian Astrologer. It was translated for the Dauphin
who was brought into England a Prisoner with his
father John, King of France, by our Edward the black prince.
In all probability, the Dauphin brought the book with him
the translation having undoubtedly been made for him
as appears by the red ink matter at the top of
the opposite page. The Translators name is on the
last leaf. The date of the MS. which appears on
the Title to the second book is 1349.

Amongst the abundantly curious matter of this MS.
is the assertion that the earth (as since proved by Sir Isaac Newton)
is flattened at the poles—a most remarkable fact to have
been stated at a period when every Astrologer affirmed that
the poles were elongated.

The Arms opposite are the Dauphins to whom
the Manuscript belonged.

He was afterwards King of France.
</insert>

<vocab>Messahalah, Masha'allah, Arabian Astrologer, Dauphin, King John II of France, Edward the Black Prince, translation, 1349, Sir Isaac Newton, polar flattening, astrology</vocab>

<image-desc>An engraved heraldic bookplate. It features a central shield with multiple quarterings, including a central inescutcheon with a lion rampant. The shield is flanked by two robed angel supporters. Atop the shield is a barred helmet with elaborate mantling, crowned with a crest of a Moor's head in profile. Below the shield, a ribbon scroll bears the Latin motto "QUI CONDUCIT". At the bottom of the plate, in very small print, is the engraver's name.</image-desc>

Exposicion de *cours vuide* est quant la lune ou la planete est vuide . la planete si est aussi come erraucie . et donc elle est dite vuide de cours . cest quant elle nest jointe a nulle planete . ou a nulle planete nest jointe a elle

Exposicion de *revenue* est quant la planete ou la lune est jointe a planete retrograde ou sous le rais du solail . si lui rent ce que elle a receu de luy . et s'ataint la cause .

Et lautre maniere de *revenue* et de *destitucion* est que la planete desvuitante cest a dire legiere soit jointe a la planete pesante et cheante et que la legiere soit cheante de l'ascendent . telle lui rent ceu quelle a receu d'elle et destruit sa disposicion ou exposicion . Et ce signefie que celle *interrogacion* n'ait point de commencement ne de fin . Donc l'exemple est que tanc soit ascendent la lune en *sagitarius v. g. degre* cheante de l'ascendent . et soit jointe a mars . et mars soit i *gemini v. xij.* cheant de l'ascendent . et donc ce signefie destruccion du *quereureument* et de la fin de ceste interrogacion . si ya autre maniere de la revenue que la planete soit legiere qui est jointe en l'angle . et soit jointe a la planete cheante de l'ascendent . donc le commencement sera de la cause quar celle qui est jointe qui signefie le commencement de la chose estoit en l'angle . et n'aura pas fin que le receveur d'elle qui signefie la fin de la chose estoit cheant . et celui qui est joint est legier et a lui est le commencement de la chose . et le receveur est celui qui est pesant est nome receveur de la disposicion ou exposicion
